BBN TCP/IP 3.0 beta, November 24, 1981 / bbnnet/tcp_input.c
SHA-256 454524176b8735098d5a53f08a1d2d86e06a24ec3309fc0b5ea19a6f8e4e8207

    1  #include "../h/param.h"
    2  #include "../h/systm.h"
    3  #include "../bbnnet/mbuf.h"
    4  #include "../bbnnet/net.h"
    5  #include "../bbnnet/ifcb.h"
    6  #include "../bbnnet/imp.h"
    7  #include "../bbnnet/ucb.h"
    8  #include "../bbnnet/tcp.h"
    9  #include "../bbnnet/ip.h"
   10  #include "../h/dir.h"
   11  #include "../h/user.h"
   12  #include "../h/inode.h"
   13  #include "../bbnnet/fsm.h"
   14  #include "../bbnnet/fsmdef.h"
   15  
   16  extern int nosum;
   17  
   18  /*
   19   * This is the scheduler for the tcp machine.  It is called
   20   * from the lower network levels, either directly from the
   21   * internet level, in case of input from the network; or
   22   * indirectly from netmain, in case of user or timer events 
   23   * which awaken the main loop.
   24   */
   25  tcp_input(mp, ip)
   26  struct mbuf *mp;
   27  struct ifcb *ip;
   28  {
   29  	register struct tcb *t;
   30  	register struct th *tp;
   31  	register struct work *p;
   32  	register struct mbuf *m;
   33  	register i, hlen, tlen;
   34  	unsigned short lport, fport;
   35  	struct ucb *up;
   36  	struct work w;
   37  	struct socket temp;
   38  	struct ucb tempucb;
   39  	struct if_local tempif;
   40  
   41  	if (mp != NULL) {       /* called directly from ip with a datagram */
   42  
   43  		/* set up needed info from ip header, note that beginning
   44  		   of tcp header struct overlaps ip header.  ip options
   45  		   have been removed by ip level option processing */
   46  
   47  		tp = (struct th *)((int)mp + mp->m_off);
   48  
   49  		/* make sure header does not overflow mbuf */
   50  
   51  		if ((hlen = tp->t_off << 2) > mp->m_len) {
   52  			printf("tcp header overflow\n");
   53  			netlog(mp);
   54  			return;
   55  		}
   56   
   57  		tlen = ((struct ip *)tp)->ip_len;
   58  		tp->t_len = short_to_net(tlen);                        
   59  		tp->t_next = NULL;
   60  		tp->t_prev = NULL;
   61  		tp->t_x1 = 0;
   62  		lport = short_from_net(tp->t_dst);
   63  		fport = short_from_net(tp->t_src);
   64  
   65  		/* do checksum calculation, drop seg if bad */
   66  
   67  #ifndef mbb
   68  		i = (unsigned short)tp->t_sum;
   69  #else
   70  		tp->t_x0 = 0;
   71  		tp->t_x00 = 0;
   72  		i = (unsigned short)short_from_net(tp->t_sum);
   73  #endif mbb
   74  		tp->t_sum = 0;
   75  
   76  		if (i != (unsigned short)cksum(mp, tlen + sizeof(struct ip))) {
   77          		netstat.t_badsum++;
   78  			if (nosum) 
   79  				goto ignore;
   80          		netlog(mp);
   81  
   82  		} else {                        
   83  ignore:
   84          		/* find a tcb for incoming message */
   85          
   86          		t = netcb.n_tcb_head;
   87          		while (t != NULL) {     /* look for exact match */
   88          
   89          			if (t->t_lport == lport &&
   90          				t->t_fport == fport &&
   91          		                t->t_ucb->uc_host.s_addr == 
   92                                                            tp->t_s.s_addr)
   93          				break;
   94          			t = t->t_tcb_next;
   95          		}
   96          
   97          		if (t == NULL) {        /* look for wild card match */
   98          
   99          		        t = netcb.n_tcb_head;
  100          			while (t != NULL) {
  101          
  102          				if (t->t_lport == lport &&
  103          				    (t->t_fport == fport ||
  104          				     t->t_fport == 0) &&
  105          				     (t->t_ucb->uc_host.s_addr == 
  106  							   tp->t_s.s_addr ||
  107          				      t->t_ucb->uc_host.s_addr == 0))
  108          		 		        break;
  109                  			t = t->t_tcb_next;
  110          			}
  111          		}
  112          
  113          		if (t != NULL) {        /* found a tcp for message */
  114  
  115  				/* byte swap header */
  116  
  117  				tp->t_len = tlen - hlen;
  118  				tp->t_src = fport;
  119  				tp->t_dst = lport;
  120  				tp->t_seq = long_from_net(tp->t_seq);
  121  				tp->t_ackno = long_from_net(tp->t_ackno);
  122  				tp->t_win = short_from_net(tp->t_win);
  123  				tp->t_urp = short_from_net(tp->t_urp);
  124  
  125  				/* do TCP option processing */
  126  
  127  				if (hlen > TCPSIZE)
  128  					tcp_opt(t, tp, hlen);
  129  
  130  			        /* check seg seq #, do RST processing */
  131  
  132  				if ((i = netprepr(t, tp)) != 0) {
  133  
  134  				/* seg failed preproc, drop it and
  135  				   possibly enter new state */
  136  
  137  					m_freem(mp);
  138  					if (i != -1)
  139  						t->t_state = i;
  140  				} else {
  141  
  142  			                /* put msg on unack queue if user 
  143  					   rcv queue is full and there is
  144  					   not a reasonable number of free
  145  					   buffers available. */
  146  			                
  147  					up = t->t_ucb;
  148  			                if ((int)up->uc_rcv - (int)up->uc_rsize <= 0
  149  					     && tp->t_len != 0
  150  					     && netcb.n_bufs < netcb.n_lowat) {
  151  			                
  152  			                	mp->m_act = (struct mbuf *)0;
  153  			                	if ((m = t->t_rcv_unack) != NULL) {
  154  			                		while (m->m_act != NULL)
  155  			                			m = m->m_act;
  156  			                		m->m_act = mp;
  157  			                	} else
  158  			                		t->t_rcv_unack = mp;
  159  			                
  160  			                } else {
  161  
  162  					/* set up work entry for seg, and call
  163  					   the fsm to process it */
  164  
  165  					        hlen += sizeof(struct ip);
  166  					        mp->m_off += hlen;
  167  					        mp->m_len -= hlen;
  168                          			w.w_tcb = t;
  169                          			w.w_dat = (char *)tp;
  170                          			action(&w, INRECV);     
  171  					}
  172  				}
  173  
  174  			} else {        /* nobody wants it */
  175  
  176          			/* free everything but the header */
  177          
  178          			m_freem(mp->m_next);
  179          			mp->m_next = NULL;
  180          			mp->m_len = sizeof(struct th);
  181          
  182          			/* form a reset from the packet and send */
  183          
  184          			temp = tp->t_d;
  185          			tp->t_d = tp->t_s;
  186          			tp->t_s = temp;
  187          			lport = tp->t_src;
  188          			tp->t_src = tp->t_dst;
  189          			tp->t_dst = lport;
  190          			if (tp->t_ack)
  191          				tp->t_seq = tp->t_ackno;
  192          			else {
  193          				tp->t_ackno = long_to_net(
  194  						       long_from_net(tp->t_seq)
  195  							+ tlen - hlen +
  196  							(tp->t_syn ? 1 : 0));
  197          				tp->t_seq = 0;
  198          			}
  199          			tp->t_rst = TRUE;
  200          			tp->t_ack = (tp->t_ack ? FALSE : TRUE);
  201          			tp->t_syn = FALSE;
  202          			tp->t_fin = FALSE;
  203          			tp->t_len = short_to_net(TCPSIZE);
  204          			tp->t_off = TCPSIZE/4;
  205  #ifndef mbb
  206  				tp->t_sum = cksum(mp, sizeof(struct th));
  207  #else
  208          			i = cksum(mp, sizeof(struct th));
  209  				tp->t_sum = short_to_net(i);
  210  #endif
  211  				tempucb.uc_host = tp->t_d;
  212  				tempucb.uc_route = NULL;
  213  				tempucb.uc_srcif = &tempif;
  214  				tempif.if_ifcb = ip;
  215          			ip_output(&tempucb, mp, TCPROTO, 
  216  					  TCPSIZE, 0, NULL);
  217          			netstat.t_badsegs++;
  218  			}
  219  		}
  220  	}
  221  
  222  	/* now look through the work list for user or timer events */
  223  
  224  	for (p = netcb.n_work; p != NULL; p = netcb.n_work) {
  225  
  226  		if ((i = p->w_type) > 0 && i < INOP)
  227  			action(p, i);
  228  
  229  		spl5();
  230  		p->w_type = 0;
  231  		netcb.n_work = p->w_next;
  232  		p->w_next = NULL;
  233  		spl0();
  234  	}
  235  	wakeup(workfree);       /* in case anyone waiting for work entries */
  236  }
  237  
  238  /*
  239   * Entry into TCP finite state machine
  240   */
  241  action(wp, input)                       
  242  register struct work *wp;
  243  int input;
  244  {
  245  	register act, newstate;
  246  	register struct tcb *tp;
  247  
  248  	tp = wp->w_tcb;
  249  
  250  	/* get index of action routine from transition table */
  251  
  252  	act = fstab[tp->t_state][input];         
  253  
  254  	/* invalid state transition, just print a message and ignore */
  255  
  256  	if (act == 0)  {
  257  		printf("tcp: bad state: tcb=%X state=%d input=%d\n",
  258  			tp, tp->t_state, input);
  259  		if (wp->w_dat != NULL)
  260  			m_freem(dtom(wp->w_dat));
  261  		return;
  262  	}
  263  
  264  	tp->net_keep = FALSE;
  265  
  266  	newstate = (*fsactab[act])(wp);         /* call action routine */
  267  
  268  	/* print debugging info for this transition if debug flag on */
  269  
  270  	if (tp->t_ucb->uc_flags & UDEBUG)
  271  		tcp_debug(tp, wp, input, newstate);
  272  
  273  #ifdef TCPDEBUG
  274  	tcp_prt(tp, input, wp->w_stype, newstate);
  275  #endif
  276  
  277  	/* handle invalid states and state transition */
  278  
  279  	if (newstate == EFAILEC) {
  280  		if (wp->w_dat != NULL)
  281  			m_freem(dtom(wp->w_dat));
  282  
  283  	/* valid transition, see if we should free net input */
  284  
  285  	} else {
  286  		if (newstate != SAME) 
  287  			tp->t_state = newstate;
  288  		if (wp->w_dat != NULL && !tp->net_keep && input == INRECV)
  289  			m_freem(dtom(wp->w_dat));
  290  	}
  291  
  292  }
  293  
  294  /*
  295   * Allocate a work list entry
  296   */
  297  w_alloc(type, stype, tp, m)     
  298  register type;
  299  int stype;
  300  struct tcb *tp;
  301  char *m;
  302  {
  303  	register struct work *w, *wp;
  304  	register char *t;
  305  	register x;
  306  
  307  	/* look for a free work entry */
  308  
  309  again:
  310  	for (w = workfree; w < workNWORK; w++)
  311  		if (w->w_type == 0) {
  312  
  313  			/* found a free entry */
  314  
  315  			x = spl5();
  316  			w->w_type = type;
  317  			w->w_stype = stype;
  318  			w->w_tcb = tp;
  319  			w->w_dat = m;
  320  
  321  			/* chain work request at end of list */
  322  
  323           		w->w_next = NULL;
  324          		
  325          		if ((wp = netcb.n_work) != NULL) {
  326          			while (wp->w_next != NULL)       
  327          				wp = wp->w_next;
  328          			wp->w_next = w;
  329          		} else 
  330          			netcb.n_work = w;
  331  			splx(x);
  332  
  333  			/* wakeup net input process */
  334  
  335  			wakeup((caddr_t)&ifcb[0]);
  336  			return;
  337  		}
  338  
  339  	/* couldn't find a free entry: wait for one if request is from user */
  340  
  341  	if (type != INRECV && type != ISTIMER && type != INCLEAR) {
  342  		sleep(workfree, PZERO);
  343  		goto again;
  344  
  345  	/* if timer request, just reschedule a second later */
  346  
  347  	} else if (type == ISTIMER) {
  348  		t = (char *)((char *)&tp->t_init + stype - 1);
  349  		*t++;
  350  
  351  	/* otherwise, just drop the request, since it can only be
  352  	   a net clear or an unacked queue recycle (regular net input
  353  	   is done with a private work entry). */
  354  
  355  	} else 
  356  		if (m != NULL) 
  357  			m_freem(dtom(m));       
  358  }
  359  
  360  /*
  361   * Write a record in the tcp debugging log
  362   */
  363  tcp_debug(tp, wp, input, newstate)   
  364  register struct tcb *tp;
  365  register struct work *wp;
  366  register input, newstate;
  367  {
  368  	struct t_debug debuf;
  369  	register struct th *n;
  370  	register struct inode *ip;
  371  	register off_t siz;
  372  
  373  	/* look for debugging file inode */
  374  
  375  	if ((ip=netcb.n_debug)==NULL)
  376  		return;
  377  	plock(ip);
  378  
  379  	/* set up the debugging record */
  380  
  381  	debuf.t_tod = (long)time;
  382  	if ((debuf.t_tcb = tp) != NULL)
  383  		debuf.t_old = tp->t_state;
  384  	else
  385  		debuf.t_old = 0;
  386  	debuf.t_inp = input;
  387  	debuf.t_tim = wp->w_stype;
  388  	debuf.t_new = newstate;
  389  
  390  	if (input == INRECV) {
  391  		n = (struct th *)wp->w_dat;
  392  		debuf.t_sno = n->t_seq;
  393  		debuf.t_ano = n->t_ackno;
  394  		debuf.t_wno = n->t_win;
  395  		if (debuf.t_new == 0xff)
  396  	        	debuf.t_lno = ((struct ip *)n)->ip_len;
  397  		else
  398  			debuf.t_lno = n->t_len;
  399  		debuf.t_flg = *(char *)((int)&n->t_win - 1);
  400  	} 
  401  
  402  	/* write out the record */
  403  
  404  	siz = ip->i_size;
  405  	u.u_offset = siz;
  406  	u.u_base = (caddr_t)&debuf;
  407  	u.u_count = sizeof(debuf);
  408  	u.u_segflg = 1;
  409  	u.u_error = 0;
  410  	writei(ip);
  411  	if(u.u_error)
  412  		ip->i_size = siz;
  413  	prele(ip);
  414  }
  415  
  416  
  417  #ifdef TCPDEBUG
  418  
  419  /*
  420   * Print debugging info on the console
  421   */
  422  tcp_prt(tp, input, timer, newstate)     
  423  register struct tcb *tp;
  424  register input, timer, newstate;
  425  {
  426  	register oldstate;
  427  
  428  	oldstate = tp->t_state;
  429  
  430  	printf("TCP(%X) %s X %s", tp, tcpstates[oldstate], tcpinputs[input]);
  431  
  432  	if (input == ISTIMER)
  433  		printf("(%s)", tcptimers[timer]);
  434  
  435  	printf(" --> %s", tcpstates[ (newstate > 0) ? newstate : oldstate ]);
  436  
  437  	if (newstate < 0)
  438  		printf(" (FAILED)\n");
  439  	else
  440  		putchar('\n');
  441  }
  442  
  443  #endif

   11  /*
   12   * TCP finite state machine primitives
   13   *
   14   * These routines are called from the procedures in tcp_procs.c to do low
   15   * level protocol functions.
   16   */
   17  
   18  /*
   19   * Send a tcp segment
   20   */
   21  send_tcp(tp, fin, syn, eol, urg, urp, len, dat)        
   22  register struct tcb *tp;
   23  char fin, syn, eol, urg;
   24  short urp;
   25  int len;
   26  struct mbuf *dat;
   27  {
   28  	register struct mbuf *m;
   29  	register struct th *t;
   30  	register struct ucb *up;
   31  	register i;
   32  	struct work w;
   33  
   34  	if ((m = m_get(1)) == NULL)
   35  		return(FALSE);
   36  	up = tp->t_ucb;
   37  
   38  	m->m_off = MSIZE - sizeof(struct th);
   39  	m->m_len = sizeof(struct th);
   40  	m->m_next = dat;
   41  
   42  	/* adjust data length for syn and fin */
   43  
   44  	if (syn)
   45  		len--;
   46  	if (fin)
   47  		len--;
   48  
   49  	t = (struct th *)((int)m + m->m_off);
   50  
   51  	t->t_s = up->uc_srcif->if_addr;
   52  	t->t_d = up->uc_host;
   53  
   54  	t->t_src = short_to_net(tp->t_lport);
   55  	t->t_dst = short_to_net(tp->t_fport);
   56  
   57  	t->t_seq = long_to_net(tp->snd_nxt);
   58  	t->t_ackno = long_to_net(tp->rcv_nxt);
   59  
   60  	t->t_fin = fin;
   61  	t->t_syn = syn & ~tp->snd_rst;
   62  	t->t_rst = tp->snd_rst;
   63  	t->t_eol = eol;
   64  	t->t_ack = tp->syn_rcvd;   
   65  	t->t_urg = urg;
   66  
   67  	i = rcv_resource(tp) * MLEN;
   68  	t->t_win = short_to_net(i);
   69  	t->t_urp = short_to_net(urp);
   70  
   71  	t->t_next = NULL;
   72  	t->t_prev = NULL;
   73  	t->t_x1 = 0;
   74  	t->t_x2 = 0;
   75  	t->t_x3 = 0;
   76  	t->t_sum = 0;
   77  	t->t_pr = TCPROTO;
   78  	t->t_len = short_to_net(len + TCPSIZE);
   79  	t->t_off = TCPSIZE/4;
   80  
   81  #ifndef mbb
   82  	t->t_sum = cksum(m, len + sizeof(struct th));
   83  #else
   84  	t->t_x0 = 0;
   85  	t->t_x00 = 0;
   86  	i = cksum(m, len + sizeof(struct th));
   87  	t->t_sum = short_to_net(i);
   88  #endif mbb
   89  
   90  	i = ip_output(up, m, TCPROTO, len+TCPSIZE, tp->t_optlen, tp->t_opts);
   91  
   92  	if (up->uc_flags & UDEBUG) {
   93  		w.w_dat = (char *)t;
   94  		w.w_stype = i;
   95  		tcp_debug(tp, &w, INRECV, -1);
   96  	}
   97  
   98  	return(i);
   99  }

  101  /*
  102   * Find the first empty spot in rcv buffer 
  103   */
  104  sequence firstempty(tp)                  
  105  register struct tcb *tp;
  106  {
  107  	register struct th *p, *q;
  108  
  109  	if ((p = tp->t_rcv_next) == (struct th *)tp || tp->rcv_nxt < p->t_seq)
  110  		return(tp->rcv_nxt);
  111  
  112  	while ((q = p->t_next) != (struct th *)tp &&    
  113  		(t_end(p) + 1) == q->t_seq)
  114  		p = q;
  115  
  116  	return(t_end(p) + 1);
  117  }
  118  
  119  /*
  120   * Get number of rcv bufs available 
  121   */
  122  rcv_resource(tp)                       
  123  register struct tcb *tp;
  124  {
  125  	register struct ucb *up;
  126  	register struct mbuf *m;
  127  	register struct th *t;
  128  	register i;
  129  
  130  	up = tp->t_ucb;                 /* -> ucb */
  131  
  132  	i = up->uc_rcv - up->uc_rsize;  /* total # rcv bufs allocated */
  133  	if (i < 0)
  134  		return(0);
  135  
  136  	/* count bufs in tcp msg rcv queue */
  137  
  138  	if (netcb.n_bufs < netcb.n_lowat)
  139  		for (t = tp->t_rcv_next; i > 0 && t != (struct th *)tp; 
  140  								t = t->t_next)
  141  			for (m = dtom(t); i > 0 && m != NULL; m = m->m_next)
  142  				i--;
  143  	return(i);
  144  }
  145  
  146  /*
  147   * TCP/IP checksum routine: one's complement of the one's complement sum of
  148   * the sixteen bit words of header/data
  149   */
  150  #ifndef mbb
  151  #define lobyte(x) (*((char *)x) & 0xff)
  152  #define hibyte(x) ((unsigned)((*((char *)x) & 0xff) << 8))
  153  #else
  154  #define lobyte(x) ((unsigned)((*((char *)x) & 0xff) << 8))
  155  #define hibyte(x) (*((char *)x) & 0xff)
  156  #endif
  157  
  158  cksum(m, len)
  159  register struct mbuf *m;
  160  register len;
  161  {
  162  	register unsigned short *w;
  163  	register unlong sum;
  164  	register mlen;
  165  	register unsigned short i;
  166  
  167  	w = (unsigned short *)((int)m + m->m_off);
  168  	mlen = m->m_len;
  169  	sum = 0;
  170  
  171  	for (; len > 0; len -= 2, mlen -= 2) {
  172  
  173  try:            if (mlen > 1) {         /* can get a word */
  174  
  175  			if (len > 1) {
  176  #ifndef mbb
  177  				sum += *(w++);
  178  #else
  179  				i = *(w++);
  180  				sum += short_from_net(i);
  181  #endif mbb
  182  
  183  			} else            /* trailing odd byte */
  184  
  185  				sum += lobyte(w);
  186  
  187  		} else if (mlen > 0) {  /* last byte of mbuf */
  188  
  189  			sum += lobyte(w);
  190  
  191  			if (len > 1) {
  192  
  193          			/* get next good mbuf for hi byte */
  194          
  195          			while ((m = m->m_next) != NULL && 
  196  					(mlen = m->m_len + 1) == 1);
  197          			if (m != NULL) {
  198          				w = (unsigned short *)((int)m + m->m_off);
  199          				sum += hibyte(w);
  200  					w = (unsigned short *)((int)w + 1);
  201          			} else
  202          				len = 0;        /* force loop exit */
  203  			}
  204  
  205  		} else {                /* end of mbuf, get next and try again */
  206  
  207  			while ((m = m->m_next) != NULL && (mlen = m->m_len) == 0);
  208  			if (m != NULL) {
  209  				w = (unsigned short *)((int)m + m->m_off);
  210  				goto try;
  211  			} else
  212  				break;
  213  		}
  214  	}
  215  
  216  	/* add in one's complement carry */
  217  
  218  	sum = (sum & 0xffff) + (sum >> 16);
  219  	sum = (sum + (sum >> 16));
  220  	return(~sum & 0xffff);
  221  }
  222  
  223  /*
  224   * Copy mbuf chain from snd buffer for sends
  225   */
  226  struct mbuf *snd_copy(tp, start, end)
  227  struct tcb *tp;
  228  sequence start, end;
  229  {
  230  	register struct mbuf *m, *n;
  231  	register sequence off;
  232  	register adj, len;
  233  	struct mbuf *top;
  234  
  235  	/* make sure we have something to copy */
  236  
  237  	if (start >= end)    
  238  		return(NULL);
  239  
  240  	off = tp->snd_una;
  241  	if (!tp->syn_acked)		/* skip over SYN */
  242  		off++;
  243  	m = tp->t_ucb->uc_sbuf;
  244  
  245  	/* find mbuf to start copying */
  246  
  247  	while (m != NULL && start >= (off + m->m_len)) {
  248  		off += m->m_len;
  249  		m = m->m_next;
  250  	}
  251  
  252  	/* get buffer to copy into */
  253  
  254  	if (m == NULL || (n = top = m_get(1)) == NULL)
  255  		return(NULL);
  256  
  257  	adj = start - off;
  258  	off = start;
  259  	goto partial;		/* start with partial copy */
  260  
  261  	do {		/* main copy loop */
  262  
  263  		/* amount we can copy into mbuf */
  264  
  265  		if ((adj = MLEN - n->m_len) >= m->m_len)
  266  			len = m->m_len;		/* can copy all */
  267  		else
  268  			len = adj;		/* partial copy */
  269  
  270  		/* copy as much as possible into mbuf */
  271  
  272  		bcopy((caddr_t)((int)m + m->m_off), 
  273  			(caddr_t)((int)n + MHEAD + n->m_len), len);
  274  		n->m_len += len;
  275  		off += len;
  276  
  277  		/* must get another mbuf to copy into */
  278  
  279  		if (end > off && adj < m->m_len) {
  280  			if ((n = n->m_next = m_get(1)) == NULL) {
  281  				m_freem(top);
  282  				return(NULL);
  283  			}
  284  
  285  			/* partial copy into new mbuf */
  286  
  287  partial:		n->m_off = MHEAD;
  288  			n->m_next = NULL;
  289  			n->m_len = m->m_len - adj;
  290  			bcopy((caddr_t)((int)m + m->m_off + adj),
  291  				(caddr_t)((int)n + MHEAD), n->m_len);
  292  			off += n->m_len;
  293  		}
  294  
  295  	} while (end > off && (m = m->m_next) != NULL);
  296  
  297  	/* make sure there was enough in buffer to copy */
  298  
  299  	if (m == NULL) {
  300  		printf("snd_copy: bad copy\n");
  301  		m_freem(top);
  302  		return(FALSE);
  303  	}
  304  
  305  	/* adjust length of last mbuf copied */
  306  
  307  	n->m_len -= (off - end);
  308  	return(top);
  309  }
  310  
  311  /*
  312   * Enqueue segment on tcp sequencing queue
  313   */
  314  tcp_enq(p, prev)
  315  register struct th *p;
  316  register struct th *prev;
  317  {
  318  	p->t_prev = prev;
  319  	p->t_next = prev->t_next;
  320  	prev->t_next->t_prev = p;
  321  	prev->t_next = p;
  322  }
  323   
  324  /*
  325   *  Dequeue segment from tcp sequencing queue
  326   */
  327  tcp_deq(p)
  328  register struct th *p;
  329  {
  330  	p->t_prev->t_next = p->t_next;
  331  	p->t_next->t_prev = p->t_prev;
  332  }
  333  
  334  /*
  335   * Cancel a timer
  336   */
  337  t_cancel(tp, timer)                     
  338  register struct tcb *tp;
  339  register timer;
  340  {
  341  	register struct work *w;
  342  	register char *t;
  343  
  344  	/* reset timer value in tcb */
  345  
  346  	t = (char *)((char *)&tp->t_init + timer - 1);
  347  	*t = 0;
  348  
  349  	/* remove any timer work entries already enqueued */
  350  
  351  	for (w = netcb.n_work; w != NULL; w = w->w_next) {
  352  		if (w->w_tcb == tp && w->w_type == ISTIMER && 
  353  				w->w_stype == timer)
  354  			w->w_type = INOP;
  355  		
  356  		/* THIS SHOULD GO AWAY */
  357  
  358  		if (w == w->w_next) 
  359  			w->w_next = NULL;
  360  	}
  361  }
  362  
  363  /*
  364   * TCP timer update routine 
  365   */
  366  tcp_timeo()                     
  367  {
  368  	register struct tcb *tp;
  369  
  370  	/* search through tcb and update active timers */
  371  
  372  	for (tp = netcb.n_tcb_head; tp != NULL; tp = tp->t_tcb_next) {
  373  
  374  		if (tp->t_init != 0)
  375  			if (--tp->t_init == 0)
  376  				w_alloc(ISTIMER, TINIT, tp, 0);
  377  
  378  		if (tp->t_rexmt != 0)
  379  			if (--tp->t_rexmt == 0)
  380  				w_alloc(ISTIMER, TREXMT, tp, 0);
  381  
  382  		if (tp->t_rexmttl != 0)
  383  			if (--tp->t_rexmttl == 0)
  384  				w_alloc(ISTIMER, TREXMTTL, tp, 0);
  385  
  386  		if (tp->t_persist != 0)
  387  			if (--tp->t_persist == 0)
  388  				w_alloc(ISTIMER, TPERSIST, tp, 0);
  389  
  390  		if (tp->t_finack != 0)
  391  			if (--tp->t_finack == 0)
  392  				w_alloc(ISTIMER, TFINACK, tp, 0);
  393  
  394  		tp->t_xmt++;
  395  
  396  	}
  397  
  398  	netcb.n_iss += ISSINCR;         /* increment iss */
  399  
  400  	timeout(tcp_timeo, 0, HZ);      /* reschedule every second */
  401  }
  402  
  403  /*
  404   * Tell user proc about change of tcp state 
  405   */
  406  to_user(up, state)              
  407  register struct ucb *up;
  408  register short state;
  409  {
  410  
  411  	/* set user state flag and awaken user proc if asleep */
  412  
  413  	up->uc_state |= state;
  414  	wakeup(up);
  415  
  416  	/* send urgent signal to user process */
  417  
  418    	if (state == UURGENT)  
  419  		psignal(up->uc_proc, SIGURG);
  420  }
  421  
  422  /*
  423   * Do TCP option processing
  424   */
  425  tcp_opt(t, tp, hlen)        
  426  register struct th *t;
  427  register struct tcb *tp;
  428  int hlen;
  429  {
  430  	register char *p;
  431  	register i;
  432  
  433  	p = (char *)((int)t + TCPSIZE);		/* -> at options */
  434  	
  435  	if ((i = hlen - TCPSIZE) > 0) {			/* any options */
  436  
  437  		while (i > 0)
  438  
  439    			switch (*p++) {
  440  			case 0:                 
  441  			case 1:                 
  442  				i--;
  443  				break;
  444  
  445  			case 2:			/* max segment size */
  446  				if (t->t_syn && !tp->syn_rcvd)
  447  					tp->t_maxseg = MIN(tp->t_maxseg,
  448  						        *(char *)((int)p+1));
  449  			default:
  450  				i -= *p;
  451  				p += *p;
  452  			}
  453                  p += i;
  454  	}
  455  }

BBN TCP/IP 3.0 beta, November 24, 1981 / doc/net.5.n
SHA-256 0814af4056c7b6e41232cbacebdedd61933788e3a20d3665b6f27adb88a14028

    1  .TH NET  5                                       
    2  .SH NAME
    3  .IX network
    4  .IX TCP
    5  .IX IP
    6  .IX "Transmission Control Protocol"
    7  .IX "Internet Protocol"
    8  tcp, ip, rawnet \- internet networking software
    9  .SH SYNOPSIS
   10  .B open ("/dev/net/net", ncon);
   11  .sp
   12  .B struct con *ncon;
   13  .sp
   14  .nf
   15  .ta \w'struct 'u +\w'unsigned  'u +\w'struct tcb *utcb; 'u
   16  .so /usr/src/sys.4/h/con.h    
   17  .fi
   18  .SH DESCRIPTION
   19  The special file
   20  .I /dev/net/net
   21  is used to access ARPANET type packet-switched networks via the DoD standard
   22  host-host Internetworking Protocols, 
   23  .B TCP
   24  (Transmission Control Protocol), and
   25  .B IP
   26  (Internet Protocol).  It also allows communication over the local network(s)
   27  to which the system is connected with "raw" packets, enabling user software to
   28  do its own communications processing.  Access to the network at this level
   29  is the most direct form of use.  It is assumed that most users will use higher
   30  level protocol programs, like 
   31  .I ftp(1)
   32  and
   33  .I telnet(1)
   34  to communicate over the network.  (This description assumes the reader is
   35  familiar with ARPANET type communications protocols.)
   36  .SH ESTABLISHING CONNECTIONS
   37  To establish a connection via TCP or IP, or to communicate with raw packets,
   38  the 
   39  .I open(2)
   40  call is given, with the usual mode argument replaced by a pointer to a
   41  connection structure, defined in
   42  .I /usr/include/con.h.
   43  The 
   44  .I c_mode
   45  field of this structure specifies what type of connection is desired
   46  (TCP, IP, or RAW), and whether or not the connection is to be active 
   47  (specifying a specific foreign host address), or passive (with no foreign
   48  address, implying that the connection will be established when any foreign
   49  process tries to communicate with the opener).  
   50  This field also specifies other attributes of raw connections (see, RAW
   51  CONNECTIONS).
   52  .PP
   53  The
   54  .I c_sbufs
   55  and 
   56  .I c_rbufs
   57  fields specify buffer allocations for the send and receive sides of the
   58  connection, respectively.  If either value is zero, the default allocation 
   59  will be used for that direction
   60  (currently 1K bytes).  The user can request up to 8K bytes each for send
   61  and receive directions by varying these parameters between 1 and 8.  
   62  .PP
   63  For IP 
   64  and RAW connections, the
   65  .I c_hi
   66  and
   67  .I c_lo
   68  fields specify a range of IP protocol numbers or local net dispatch numbers
   69  (e.g., ARPANET link numbers) to watch for (see, RAW CONNECTIONS).  
   70  .PP
   71  The
   72  .I c_timeo
   73  parameter specifies a length of time in seconds to wait for connection
   74  establishment before aborting (this does not apply to passive opens).  If
   75  the field is zero, the default of 30 seconds is used.  
   76  .PP
   77  The remaining fields
   78  specify local, and foreign ports for TCP, and foreign and local host addresses 
   79  in network address format (see
   80  .I libnet(3)).
   81  The local port may be zero, in which case TCP assigns a unique port number to
   82  the connection.  The foreign port and host address may only be zero for a
   83  passive open.
   84  The local host address may be zero, in which case a default local address
   85  is chosen by the software.  On machines with multiple network interfaces
   86  (and network addresses), this field may be used to specify which
   87  interface/address to use.  It is an error to specify an unsupported local
   88  address.
   89  .SH READING AND WRITING
   90  If the open succeeds, a file descriptor is returned which may be used in
   91  subsequent reads and writes (see,
   92  .I read(2), write(2)).
   93  Reads and writes work as usual with a few
   94  exceptions.  A read may return with error condition ENETSTAT, which indicates
   95  that some exceptional condition has been detected.  
   96  Further status may be determined with
   97  .I ioctl(2).
   98  (see, NETWORK STATUS).  If the condition is non-fatal, the read may be
   99  re-issued.  Reads may return less data than requested, 
  100  and will block if there is no data for the user.  Writes block if
  101  the amount of send buffer resources for the connection is exceeded.  
  102  Reads will return zero only when the foreign peer has closed the connection and
  103  there is no remaining data to return to the user.  
  104  .PP
  105  In addition to normal TCP reads and writes, the user may wish to indicate EOL
  106  and URGENT data on writes and receive notification of URGENT data sent by the
  107  foreign peer.  EOL and URGENT are enabled by issueing the NETSETE or NETSETU
  108  ioctl calls.
  109  Once set, EOL is sent at the last byte of each subsequent write.  Similarly,
  110  the URGENT pointer is set to start at the first byte of the next write, and
  111  ends with the first byte sent after URGENT mode is disabled.  These modes are
  112  disabled by the NETRSETE and NETRSETU ioctl calls.  URGENT data is
  113  indicated by signal SIGURG when the first byte is received.  This signal is
  114  normally ignored.  (A status flag is also set in the presence of urgent data.)
  115  .SH CLOSING CONNECTIONS 
  116  Normally, the 
  117  .I close(2)
  118  call is used to close a TCP, IP, or RAW connection.  In each case, it indicates
  119  that the user will send or receive no more data.  For TCP connections, close
  120  initiates the connection closing protocol, though it returns immediately.
  121  Thus, the internal connection structures persist until the connection has
  122  reached the CLOSED state.  For IP and RAW connections, the close is immediate
  123  and deletes all internal structures.  
  124  .PP
  125  In addition to close for TCP connections, there is an ioctl call, NETCLOSE,
  126  which indicates that the local connection will send no more data, but is still
  127  able to receive data from the foreign peer.  In this case, subsequent writes
  128  are illegal and will terminate with errors, but subsequent reads will work
  129  until the connection is closed by the foreign peer.
  130  .SH RAW CONNECTIONS
  131  .PP
  132  Raw IP and local network protocol packets may be sent and received by
  133  specifying CONIP or CONRAW in the mode field of the open call.  These types
  134  of opens do not set up connections in the sense of TCP opens (i.e., they do
  135  not cause communication of a connection opening protocol), but the
  136  resulting channel will be referred to as a "connection" for the sake of
  137  brevity.  
  138  .PP
  139  To send raw IP or local network protocol packets, a connection is opened
  140  and writes are issued on the resulting file descriptor.  The user may
  141  specify parameters in the open call (such as destination host address, link
  142  or protocol number) that are used by the software to construct leaders for
  143  the data (RAWCOMP mode), or he may opt to supply the leaders (RAWASIS
  144  mode).  A third option exists for IP connections, where the system fills in
  145  the checksum field in the supplied leader (RAWVER mode).  In each case, one
  146  packet is sent out for each write.  Writes do not block, and return as soon
  147  as the packet has been queued for sending to the local network.  If for
  148  some reason, the packet cannot be sent (the selected network interface is
  149  unavailable), an error will be returned.
  150  .PP
  151  To receive raw IP or local network protocol packets, the user specifies a
  152  range of link or protocol numbers and a foreign and local host address in
  153  the open call.  The range is specified in the
  154  .I c_hi
  155  and
  156  .I c_lo
  157  fields.  The range must be unique (i.e., may not overlap other protocol or
  158  link numbers in use), and 
  159  .I c_lo 
  160  must be less than or equal to 
  161  .I c_hi, 
  162  or an
  163  ENETRNG error results.  If the foreign or local host addresses are
  164  specified, only messages in the specified range with the corresponding
  165  source and/or destination address are queued for the user.  If the
  166  addresses are zero, all messages falling into the specified range are
  167  returned.  Reads return no more than one packet, though they may return
  168  less if a length shorter than the packet length is specified (subsequent
  169  reads will return the remainder of the packet).  The entire packet,
  170  including the header is returned.  If the read buffer limit is exceeded,
  171  subsequent incoming messages in the specified range will be dropped.  Reads
  172  block if there is no data to satisfy the request.  The user may optionally
  173  receive ICMP or local network protocol error and control messages
  174  associated with the range by specifying RAWERR mode in the open call.  
  175  .SH NETWORK STATUS
  176  There are several 
  177  .I ioctl(2)
  178  calls available for receiving network status information or initiating certain
  179  modes or functions.  Most of these calls have been described above.  The status
  180  call, NETGETS, takes a status buffer pointer, which points to a netstate
  181  structure, illustrated above, which is filled in by the call.  
  182  .PP
  183  To summarize, the various ioctl calls are:
  184  .IP NETGETS 10
  185  Return network status information to the structure pointed at by third argument
  186  of ioctl.
  187  .IP NETSETU 10
  188  Set urgent mode starting at next byte written (TCP only).
  189  .IP NETRSETU 10
  190  Reset urgent mode, urgent pointer ends at last byte written (TCP only).
  191  .IP NETSETE 10
  192  Set EOL mode, send EOL at last byte of each subsequent write (TCP only).
  193  .IP NETRSETE 10
  194  Terminate EOL mode (TCP only).
  195  .IP NETCLOSE 10
  196  Start TCP connection close.  User can continue to receive data (TCP only).
  197  .IP NETABORT 10
  198  Abort TCP connection.  Foreign peer is reset and no more data may be sent or
  199  received (TCP only).
  200  .IP NETTCPOPT 10
  201  Set up an IP option string for a TCP connection.  The third parameter is a
  202  pointer to a structure containing a pointer to the option string and the
  203  length of the option string in bytes.   The option string is appended to the IP
  204  leader of each subsequent TCP segment sent.  If the third parameter is zero, the
  205  previous options are cancelled and no options will be sent on subsequent
  206  packets.  The options are
  207  specified in the Internet Protocol description, and are sent as is, with no
  208  verification.  The option length may not exceed 40 bytes. (TCP only).
  209  .PP
  210  In addition, there is another class of ioctl calls that are associated with
  211  network control functions and not with any specific connection.  These calls
  212  may be issued from normal TCP, IP, or RAW connections.  There is also a special
  213  control only connection which may be opened (with CONCTL passed to open, all
  214  other fields are ignored).  This type of connection exists solely for the
  215  purpose of issuing control ioctls without involving any network resources.  The
  216  control ioctls are:
  217  .IP NETSETD 10
  218  Reset the debugging log to the file name pointed at by the third
  219  argument.  The file must already exist.
  220  If the argument is zero, turn off debug logging (see, DEBUGGING). Only the
  221  super-user may issue this call.
  222  .IP NETRESET 10
  223  Cause the TCP connection specified by the tcb address given as the third
  224  argument to be reset as if a connection clear request had been received from
  225  the network.  Only the super-user may issue this call.
  226  .IP NETDEBUG 10
  227  Toggle the debugging flag for the TCP connection specified by the TCB address
  228  given as the third argument.
  229  .IP NETGINIT 10
  230  Cause the gateway initialization file
  231  .I /etc/net/gateway
  232  to be read by the kernel, and the internal gateway forwarding table to be
  233  reinitialized.  This file is in binary format and is generated from an
  234  ASCII gateway table with
  235  .I gateway(1).
  236  .SH DEBUGGING
  237  .IX "\*itrpt\*r usage"
  238  .IX "\*i/etc/net/tcpdebug\*r"
  239  .IX "network debugging"
  240  The network software enables certain trace information to be recorded for TCP
  241  connections.  This information is logged in a single debugging log file.  To
  242  enable this feature, the CONDEBUG bit in the 
  243  .I c_mode 
  244  field of the open connection parameter structure must be set, or the NETDEBUG
  245  ioctl call must issued.  The default debugging log is 
  246  .I /etc/net/tcpdebug.
  247  This may be changed or the feature may be disabled system wide with the NETSETD
  248  ioctl call.  Only the super-user may do this.  The format of the debugging
  249  information is several bytes of binary data per TCP transaction.  The log may
  250  be printed in readable form with
  251  .I trpt(1).
  252  .SH DIAGNOSTICS
  253  The following system error codes may be returned by network system calls:
  254  .IP ENETSTAT 10
  255  (35) Network status available (not a fatal error, see READS AND WRITES).
  256  .IP ENETDOWN 10
  257  (36) Open failed because specified network interface is unavailable.
  258  .IP ENETCON 10
  259  (37) Open failed because there were too many connections.
  260  .IP ENETBUF 10
  261  (38) Open, write, or ioctl failed because there was insufficient network 
  262  buffer space.  
  263  .IP ENETPARM 10
  264  (39) Network parameter error.  On open, the mode field of the connection 
  265  parameter structure is bad.  On ioctl, an unrecognized network ioctl type 
  266  was specified.
  267  .IP ENETRNG 10
  268  (40) IP or RAW open failed because the protocol or dispatch number was out of
  269  range or already in use.  TCP open failed because the user tried to open an
  270  already existing connection (i.e., one with the identical foreign host address
  271  and local and foreign ports).  NETDEBUG or NETRESET ioctls failed because the
  272  connection with the specified TCB could not be found.
  273  .IP ENETDED 10
  274  (41) TCP open failed because the foreign host is dead.
  275  .IP ERAWBAD 10
  276  (42) IP or RAW write failed, packet was not sent.
  277  .IP ENETERR 10
  278  (43) Open failed for unknown reason.  TCP read or write was attempted on a 
  279  closed connection.
  280  .IP ENETREF 10
  281  (44) TCP open failed because the destination host refused the connection.
  282  .IP ENETUNR 10
  283  (45) TCP open failed because the destination host was unreachable.
  284  .IP ENETTIM 10
  285  (46) TCP open failed because the foreign host did not respond on the specified
  286  socket within the timeout period defined in the open request.
  287  .SH FILES
  288  /dev/net/net
  289  .br
  290  /etc/net/tcpdebug
  291  .br
  292  /etc/net/gateway
  293  .SH SEE ALSO

  295  ioctl(2), libnet(3)
